	for $\ell\to\infty$ up to a subsequence (for which we use the same label) and the existence of $h_{V_t}\in L^2(\| V_t\|;\mathbb{R}^n)$ (the $L^2$ regularity follows from \cref{in1:GenMean} with $\phi\equiv 1$) such that $\delta V_t(g)=-\int g\cdot h_{V_t}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t \|$ for all $g\in C_c^1(\mathbb{R}^n;\mathbb{R}^n)$. Since $\| V_t \|$ is compactly supported, we actually have $h_{V_t}\in L^1(\| V_t\|;\mathbb{R}^n)$, hence $\delta V_t$ is a vector-valued Radon measure (in particular, its total variation is a Radon measure). Therefore, it remains to show that
	\begin{equation*}
		\Theta^{*1}(\| V_t \|=\mu_t,x)>0
	\end{equation*}
	for $\| V_t \|$-a.e.\ $x\in\mathbb{R}^n$. Let $x\in\textup{supp}(\| V_t\|)$. First, we claim that for each $\ell\in\mathbb{N}$ there is $x_\ell\in X_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}$ such that $x_\ell\to x$ (maybe after restricting to another subsequence). Assume that the converse is true, there are $\ell_0\in\mathbb N$ and $\delta\in(0,1)$ with 
	\begin{equation*}
		X_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\cap B_\delta(x)=\emptyset
	\end{equation*}
	for all $\ell\geq \ell_0$. Since $x\in \textup{supp}(\| V_t\|)$, $B_\delta(x)$ open, and $V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\stackrel{*}{\rightharpoonup} V_t$,
	\begin{multline*}
		0<\| V_t\|(B_\delta(x))\leq\liminf_\ell\|  V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\|(B_\delta(x))\\
		=\liminf_\ell\|  V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\|((S_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\setminus X_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}})\cap B_\delta(x))+\|  V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\|(X_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\cap B_\delta(x))=0,
	\end{multline*}
	where the last equality follows from \cref{Lemma1}\footnote{We can assume that the subsequence is such that $-\Delta_{j_{k_{\ell}}}|T_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)\leq c(t)\Delta t_{j_{k_{\ell}}}$ (\cref{Prop6.4}). Further, the beginning of the proof of \cref{Prop6.1} gives $\Delta t_{j_{k_{\ell}}}\leq \varepsilon_{j_{k_{\ell}}}^{c_1}<j_{k_{\ell}}^{-6c_1}=j_{k_{\ell}}^{-6(3n+20)}$.} and $X_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\cap B_\delta(x)=\emptyset$ for $\ell\geq \ell_0$. This yields a contradiction. Now let $r\leq c_2(t)$ be arbitrary (recall \cref{LowerBound}). Since eventually $\overline B_{r/2}(x_\ell)\subset\overline B_r(x)$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac{\| V_t\|(\overline B_r(x)) }{r}\geq\limsup_{\ell}\frac{\| V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\|(\overline B_r(x)) }{r}\geq \limsup_{\ell}\frac{\| V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\|(\overline B_{r/2}(x_\ell)) }{r}\geq\frac{2\lambda}{11}
	\end{equation*}
	by $\| V_t^{j_{k_{\ell}}}\|\stackrel{*}{\rightharpoonup} \| V_t \|$ and \cref{LowerBound}. Hence, we get 
	\begin{equation*}
		\Theta^{*1}(\| V_t \|,x)\geq \frac{\lambda}{11}
	\end{equation*}
	for all $x\in\textup{supp}(\| V_t \|)$, which shows the applicability of \cref{AllardRect}.
	By \cite[Sec.\ 3.5, Thm.\ 1]{A72} and \cite[Sec.\ 2.8, Thm.\ 5]{A72} we have
	\begin{equation}
		\label{9.22}
		\int\psi(x,P)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P)=\int\psi(x,\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t \|,x))\Theta^1(\| V_t \|,x)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} \mathcal{H}^1(x)=\int\psi(x,\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t \|,x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} \| V_t\|(x)
	\end{equation}
	for all $\psi\in C_c(\mathcal G_1(\mathbb{R}^n))$. 
	This indicates that the varifold $V_t$ is uniquely characterized by its weight measure $\| V_t \|=\mu_t$ (recall that $\mu_t$ was obtained without passing to a subsequence of $(j_k)$).
	\Cref{LowerBound} can actually be applied to any subsequence of $(j_k)$ yielding another subsequence $(j_{k_\ell})$ which can be further refined as in this proof.
	In particular, every subsequence of $(j_k)$ admits a subsequence  $(j_{k_\ell})$ such that $V_t$ equals the limit (in the weak-$*$ sense) of the corresponding induced varifolds.
	Therefore, the whole sequence $(V_t^{j_{k}})$ satisfies $V_t^{j_{k}}\stackrel{*}{\rightharpoonup} V_t$.
	Finally, \cref{in1:timeIntmean} follows from the beginning of the proof of \cref{LowerBound}. Indeed, for a.e.\ $t\in\mathbb{R}_+$ there is a subsequence $N_t:\mathbb N\to \{ j_k\:|\: k\in\mathbb N \}$ with 
	\begin{equation*}
		\lim_\ell\int\frac{ |\Psi_{\varepsilon_{N_t(\ell)}}\ast\delta_\tau T_t^{N_t(\ell)}|^2 }{\Psi_{\varepsilon_{N_t(\ell)}}\ast|T_t^{N_t(\ell)}|_\tau +\varepsilon_{N_t(\ell)}}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal L^n=	\liminf_k\int\frac{ |\Psi_{\varepsilon_{j_{k}}}\ast\delta_\tau T_t^{j_{k}}|^2 }{\Psi_{\varepsilon_{j_{k}}}\ast|T_t^{j_{k}}|_\tau +\varepsilon_{j_{k}}}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal L^n
	\end{equation*}
	and such that \cref{in1:GenMean} is satisfied with $V=V_t$ and $m\mapsto i_m$ replaced by $\ell\mapsto N_t(\ell)$.
	This gives (using $\phi\equiv 1$ and recalling \cref{in1:6.19}).
	\begin{equation*}
		\int_{[0,R]}\int |h_{V_t}|^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\|\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t)\leq \int_{[0,R]}\liminf_\ell \int\frac{ |\Psi_{\varepsilon_{N_t(\ell)}}\ast\delta_\tau T_t^{N_t(\ell)}|^2 }{\Psi_{\varepsilon_{N_t(\ell)}}\ast|T_t^{N_t(\ell)}|_\tau +\varepsilon_{N_t(\ell)}}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal L^n\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t)<\infty
	\end{equation*}
	for all $R\in\mathbb{R}_+$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
First, take $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb{R}^n;\mathbb{R}_+)$ independent of $t$. 
	We can assume $\phi<1$ in $\mathbb{R}^n$ by rescaling the inequality in the statement.
	Let $i\in\mathbb N$ such that $\hat\phi=\phi+1/i<1$.
	We have $\min\hat\phi\in\mathbb{R}_+^*$. Hence, there is $p\in\mathbb N$ with $\hat\phi\in\mathcal F_p^+$.
	Fix $t_1,t_2\in\mathbb{R}_+$ with $t_1<t_2$. Let $k$ sufficiently large such that $j_k>\max\{ p,t_2 \}$ and $\Delta t_{j_k}<t_2-t_1$.
	There exist $N_1=N_1(k),N_2=N_2(k)\in\mathbb N$ with $t_1\in ( (N_1-2)\Delta t_{j_k},(N_1-1)\Delta t_{j_k} ]$ and $t_2\in ( (N_2-1)\Delta t_{j_k},N_2\Delta t_{j_k} ]$.
	By \cref{in1:6.5} we have (cf.\ \cite[(9.6)]{KT17})
	\begin{equation}
		\label[ineq]{in:9.6}
		|T^{j_k,\ell}|_\tau(\hat\phi)-|T^{j_k,\ell-1}|_\tau(\hat\phi)\leq\Delta t_{j_k}\left( \delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8} \right)
	\end{equation}
	for all $\ell=1,\ldots,j_k2^{p_{j_k}}$. 
	Summation over $\ell=N_1,\ldots,N_2$ yields (see \cite[(9.7)]{KT17})
	\begin{equation}
		\label[ineq]{in1:9.7}
		|T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\hat\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\hat\phi)\leq\sum_{\ell=N_1}^{N_2}\Delta t_{j_k}\left( \delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8} \right).
	\end{equation}
	Using the definition of $\hat\phi $ and \cref{in1:6.3}, we can estimate (\cite[(9.8)]{KT17})
	\begin{multline*}
		|T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\hat\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\hat\phi)\geq|T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\phi)-\frac1i|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)\\
		\geq |T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\phi)-\frac1i( |T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8}(N_1-1)\Delta t_{j_k} ),
	\end{multline*}
	hence
	\begin{multline}
		\limsup_k|T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\hat\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\hat\phi)\geq\limsup_k |T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\phi)-\frac1i |T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)\\
		=\| V_{t_2} \|(\phi)-\| V_{t_1} \|(\phi)-\frac1i |T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n).\label[ineq]{in1:9.9}
	\end{multline}
	Now abbreviate $T=\theta\mathcal{H}^1\mathbin{\vrule height 1.3ex depth 0pt width 0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1ex} S=T^{j_k,\ell},h=h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell})$, and $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{j_k}$.
	Recall \cref{TauVariation,phiWeightedTauVariation}\footnote{Note that $\delta_\tau T(\hat\phi h)=\int\hat\phi\textup{div}_S(h)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau+\int h^\intercal P\nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau=\delta_\tau(T,\hat\phi)(h)-\int\nabla\hat\phi^\intercal  h\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau+\int h^\intercal P \nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} |T|_\tau=\delta_\tau(T,\hat\phi)(h)-\int h^\intercal P^\perp \nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau$, cf.\ calculation in \cite[(2.5)]{KT17}.},
	\begin{equation}
		\label{9.10}
		\delta_\tau(T,\hat\phi)(h)=\delta_\tau T(\hat\phi h)+\int h^\intercal P^\perp \nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau,
	\end{equation}
	where $P=P(x)$ denotes the projection onto $T_xS$ for $\mathcal{H}^1$-a.e.\ $x\in S$.
	Applying the Cauchy\textendash Schwarz inequality to $\int h^\intercal P^\perp \nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau$ yields (cf.\ \cite[(9.13)]{KT17})
	\begin{equation*}
		\int h^\intercal P^\perp \nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau\leq \left( \int \frac{|\nabla\hat\phi|^2}{\hat\phi}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau \right)^{1/2}\left( \int \hat\phi|h|^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau \right)^{1/2}.
	\end{equation*}
	By definition of $\hat\phi$, \cref{in1:6.27}, and $\hat\phi\in\mathcal F_p^+$ we get
	\begin{equation*}
		\frac{|\nabla\hat\phi|^2}{\hat\phi}\leq \frac{|\nabla\phi|^2}{\phi}\leq 2||\nabla^2\phi|_2|_\infty.
	\end{equation*}
	This gives
	\begin{equation*}
		\left( \int \frac{|\nabla\hat\phi|^2}{\hat\phi}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau \right)^{1/2}\leq \sqrt{2}||\nabla^2\phi|_2|_\infty^{1/2}|T|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)^{1/2}.
	\end{equation*}
	Abbreviating $\hat I=\int\frac{\hat\phi|\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast\delta_\tau T|^2}{\Psi_{\varepsilon}\ast|T|_\tau+\varepsilon}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}^n$, we obtain (using \cref{in1:5.24})
	\begin{equation*}
		\int h^\intercal P^\perp \nabla\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T|_\tau\leq c(\nabla^2\phi)|T|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)^{1/2}\left( (1+\varepsilon^{1/4})\hat I+\varepsilon^{1/4} \right)^{1/2}.
	\end{equation*}
	\Cref{in1:5.23} yields $|\delta_\tau T(\hat\phi h)+\hat I|\leq \varepsilon^{1/4}(\hat I+1)$, hence (revoking the abbreviations $T=T^{j_k,\ell},h=h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell})$, and $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{j_k}$)
	\begin{equation*}
		\delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))\leq \varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/4}(\hat I+1)-\hat I+c(\nabla^2\phi)|T^{j_k,\ell}|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)\left( (1+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/4})\hat I+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/4} \right)^{1/2}.
	\end{equation*}
	Now it is not difficult to see that this implies that $\sup_{\ell=N_1-1,\ldots,N_2+1}\delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))$ is bounded by some constant $A=A(|T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n),t_2,\nabla^2\phi)\in\mathbb{R}_+$ (using also \cref{in1:6.3,in1:6.7}), see \cite[(9.14)]{KT17}\footnote{Indeed, we have $ \delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))\leq \varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/4}(\hat I+1)-\hat I+c(|T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n),\nabla^2\phi)( \hat I^{1/2}+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8}\hat I^{1/2}+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8} )$ and the functions (depending on $x$) $\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/4}x-x/3,c(|T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n),\nabla^2\phi)x^{1/2}-x/3,$ and $c(|T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n),\nabla^2\phi)\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8}x^{1/2}-x/3$ are bounded on any compact interval in $\mathbb{R}_+$ for $k$ sufficiently large.}. This gives (see \cite[(9.15)]{KT17})
	\begin{multline}
		\limsup_k \sum_{\ell=N_1}^{N_2}\Delta t_{j_k} \delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))\leq\limsup_k\int_{[t_1,t_2+\Delta t_{j_k}]}  \delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t)\\
		=\limsup_k\int_{[t_1,t_2]}  \delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t)\leq \int_{[t_1,t_2]}  \limsup_k\delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t),\label[ineq]{in1:9.15}
	\end{multline}
	where (reversed) Fatou's lemma and the bound $A$ were used in the last inequality. Next, we use the same calculations as in \cite[(9.16) \& (9.17)]{KT17} applied to the integrand in the latter integral. Fix any $t\in[t_1,t_2]\setminus \mathcal N$, where $\mathcal{L}$-zero set $\mathcal N\subset [t_1,t_2]$ is chosen such that the subsequent conclusions hold. Let $(j_{k_m})\subset (j_k)$ be a subsequence such that $\lim_m\delta_\tau(T_t^{j_{k_m}},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}}))=\limsup_k\delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))$. By \cref{9.10} we have
	\begin{multline*}
		\lim_m -\delta_\tau T_t^{j_{k_m}}(\hat\phi h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}}))-\int h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})^\intercal (P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi \textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau\\
		=\liminf_k-\delta_\tau T_t^{j_k}(\hat\phi h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))-\int h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k})^\intercal (P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi \textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_k}|_\tau.
	\end{multline*}
	Again, by the two estimates which were previously used to bound \cref{9.10}, the right-hand side is bounded from above by $\liminf_k 2\hat I_t^{j_k}+A$, and the left-hand side can be estimated from below by $\limsup_m \hat I_t^{j_{k_m}}-A$\footnote{Formally, $-\delta_\tau-\int=-(\delta_\tau+\hat I+\int)+\hat I\geq -A+\hat I$.}. This gives
	\begin{equation*}
		\limsup_m \hat I_t^{j_{k_m}}\leq \liminf_k 2\hat I_t^{j_k}+2A.
	\end{equation*}
	Define $f(t)=\liminf_k 2\hat I_t^{j_k}+2A$. Note that $f(t)<\infty$ (\cref{Prop6.4} and Fatou's lemma). Recall that $\hat\phi\geq 1/i$, thus (cf.\ \cite[(9.19)]{KT17})
	\begin{equation}
		\label[ineq]{in1:9.19}
		\limsup_m\int\frac{|\Psi_{\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}\ast\delta_\tau T_t^{j_{k_m}}|^2}{\Psi_{\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}\ast|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau+\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}^n \leq \limsup_m i\hat I_t^{j_{k_m}}\leq if(t)<\infty.
	\end{equation}
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
                                                                                                                       
	By $|\delta_\tau T(\hat\phi h)+\hat I|\leq \varepsilon^{1/4}(\hat I+1)$, the above, $f(t)<\infty$, and \cref{Prop5.6} we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\limsup_m\delta_\tau T_t^{j_{k_m}}(\hat\phi h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}}))\leq \limsup_m -\hat I_t^{j_{k_m}}= -\liminf_m \hat I_t^{j_{k_m}}\leq-\int |h_{ V_t}|^2\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\|  V_t \|.
	\end{equation*}
	Using \cref{9.10} this gives (see \cite[(9.21)]{KT17})
	\begin{multline}
		\limsup_k\delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))=\lim_m\delta_\tau(T_t^{j_{k_m}},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}}))\\
		\leq-\int |h_{ V_t}|^2\hat\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\|  V_t \|+\limsup_m\int h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})^\intercal (P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi \textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau.\label[ineq]{in1:9.21}
	\end{multline}
	Let $\varepsilon>0$ be arbitrary.
	Recall that (\cref{9.22})
	\begin{equation}
		\label{9.22recall}
		\int\psi(x,P)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P)=\int\psi(x,\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t \|,x))\Theta^1(\| V_t \|,x)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} \mathcal{H}^1(x)=\int\psi(x,\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t \|,x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} \| V_t\|(x)
	\end{equation}
	for all $\psi\in C_c(\mathcal G_1(\mathbb{R}^n))$. 
	By density there is $g\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb{R}^n;\mathbb{R}^n)$ with
	\begin{equation}
		\label[ineq]{in1:9.23}
		\int| \textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t\|,x)^\perp\nabla\hat\phi(x)-g(x) |^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\|(x)<\varepsilon^2.
	\end{equation}
	Write \cite[(9.24)]{KT17}
	\begin{multline}
		\label{9.24}
		\int h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})^\intercal (P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi \textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau=\underbrace{\int ((P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi -g)\cdot h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau}_{E_1}\\
		+\underbrace{\int g\cdot h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau+\delta_\tau T_t^{j_{k_m}}(g)}_{E_2}+\underbrace{-\delta_\tau T_t^{j_{k_m}}(g)+\delta V_t(g)}_{E_3}\\
		+\underbrace{\int h_{V_t}\cdot(g-\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t\|,\cdot)^\perp\nabla\hat\phi)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\|}_{E_4}+\int h_{V_t}(x)\cdot (P^\perp\nabla\hat\phi(x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P).
	\end{multline}
	Using the Cauch\textendash Schwarz inequality together with \cref{9.22recall,in1:9.23,in1:9.19,in1:GenMean}, we get \cite[(9.27)]{KT17}
	\begin{equation}
		\label[ineq]{in1:9.27}
		|E_1|\leq \left(  \int |(P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi -g|^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau\right)^{1/2}\left( \int |h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})|^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau \right)^{1/2}\leq\varepsilon (if(t))^{1/2}.
	\end{equation}
	Application of \cref{Prop5.5,in1:9.19} yields
	\begin{equation}
		\label{9.28}
		\lim_m|E_2|=0.
	\end{equation}
	Property $v(\tau(|\theta_t^{j_{k_m}}|),S_t^{j_{k_m}})\stackrel{*}{\rightharpoonup} V_t$ gives
	\begin{equation}
		\label{9.29}
		\lim_m|E_3|=0.
	\end{equation}
	Again, invoking the Cauchy\textendash Schwarz inequality together with \cref{in1:GenMean,in1:9.19,in1:9.23}, we obtain \cite[(9.30)]{KT17}
	\begin{multline}
		\label[ineq]{in1:9.30}
		|E_4|\leq \int |h_{V_t}||g-\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t\|,\cdot)^\perp\nabla\hat\phi|\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\|\leq \left( \int |h_{V_t}|^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\| \right)^{1/2}\left( \int |g-\textup{Tan}^1(\| V_t\|,\cdot)^\perp\nabla\hat\phi|^2\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\| \right)^{1/2}\\
		\leq\varepsilon (if(t))^{1/2}.
	\end{multline}
	                                                                                                                                   
	In summary, \cref{in1:9.27}, \cref{9.28,9.29}, and \cref{in1:9.30} give (letting $\varepsilon\to 0$) \cite[(9.32)]{KT17}
	\begin{equation}
		\label[ineq]{in1:9.32}
		\limsup_m \int h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_{k_m}}}(T_t^{j_{k_m}})^\intercal (P_t^{j_{k_m}})^\perp\nabla\hat\phi \textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}|T_t^{j_{k_m}}|_\tau\leq \int h_{V_t}(x)\cdot (P^\perp\nabla\phi(x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P),
	\end{equation}
	where we also used $\nabla\hat\phi=\nabla\phi$.
	Using \cref{in1:9.32} in \cref{in1:9.21} and $\phi\leq\hat \phi$ gives \cite[(9.33)]{KT17}
	\begin{equation}
		\limsup_k\delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))\leq-\int |h_{ V_t}|^2\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\|  V_t \|+\int h_{V_t}(x)\cdot (P^\perp\nabla\phi(x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P).\label[ineq]{in1:9.33}
	\end{equation}
	                                                           
	                 
	                       
	                                                                                       
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
	               
	\Cref{in1:9.9,in1:9.7,in1:9.15,in1:9.33} imply
	\begin{multline*}
		\| V_{t_2} \|(\phi)-\| V_{t_1} \|(\phi)-\frac1i |T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)\leq\limsup_k |T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\phi)-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\phi)-\frac{1}{i}|T_0|_\tau(\mathbb{R}^n)\\
		\leq \limsup_k\sum_{\ell=N_1}^{N_2}\Delta t_{j_k}(\delta_\tau(T^{j_k,\ell},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8})\leq\int_{[t_1,t_2]}\limsup_k \delta_\tau(T_t^{j_k},\hat\phi)(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t)\\
		\leq\int_{[t_1,t_2]} -\int |h_{V_t}|^2\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\|+\int h_{V_t}(x)\cdot (P^\perp\nabla\phi(x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t). 
	\end{multline*}
	Letting $i\to\infty$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
		\| V_{t_2} \|(\phi)-\| V_{t_1} \|(\phi) \leq-\int_{[t_1,t_2]}\int |h_{V_t}|^2\phi\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\| V_t\|\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t)+\int_{[t_1,t_2]}\int h_{V_t}(x)\cdot (P^\perp\nabla\phi(x))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}} V_t(x,P)\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t),
	\end{equation*}
	which shows the statement for time-independent $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb{R}^n;\mathbb{R}_+)$.
	Now assume that $\phi=\phi(x,t)\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{R}_+;\mathbb{R}_+)$.
	Again, we can assume that $\phi\in(0,1)$ and define $\tilde\phi=\phi+1/i<1$ for sufficiently large $i\in\mathbb N$.
	Then, as before, there is $q\in\mathbb N $ such that $\tilde\phi\in\mathcal F_q^+$ for all $t\in\mathbb{R}_+$. 
	We may choose $k$ sufficiently large such that $j_k>\max\{ q,t_2 \}$.
	Application of \cref{in1:6.5} gives (cf.\ \cref{in:9.6})
	\begin{multline*}
		|T^{j_k,\ell}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))-|T^{j_k,\ell-1}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,(\ell-1)\Delta t_{j_k}))\\
		=|T^{j_k,\ell}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))-|T^{j_k,\ell-1}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))+|T^{j_k,\ell-1}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))-|T^{j_k,\ell-1}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,(\ell-1)\Delta t_{j_k}))\\
		\leq \Delta t_{j_k}(\delta_\tau (T^{j_k,\ell},\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8})+|T^{j_k,\ell-1}|_\tau(\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k})-\phi(.,(\ell-1)\Delta t_{j_k}))
	\end{multline*}
	for all $\ell=1,\ldots,j_k2^{p_{j_k}}$.
	As in \cref{in1:9.7}, we take the telescope sum,
	\begin{multline*}
		|T^{j_k,N_2}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,N_2\Delta t_{j_k}))-|T^{j_k,N_1-1}|_\tau(\tilde\phi(.,(N_1-1)\Delta t_{j_k}))\\
		\leq \sum_{\ell=N_1}^{N_2}\Delta t_{j_k}(\delta_\tau (T^{j_k,\ell},\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8})+\sum_{r=N_1}^{N_2}|T^{j_k,r-1}|_\tau(\phi(.,r\Delta t_{j_k})-\phi(.,(r-1)\Delta t_{j_k})).
	\end{multline*}
	Applying $\limsup_k$ to the last term yields (applying the calculation in \cite[(9.36)]{KT17})
	\begin{equation*}
		\limsup_k \sum_{r=N_1}^{N_2}|T^{j_k,r-1}|_\tau(\phi(.,r\Delta t_{j_k})-\phi(.,(r-1)\Delta t_{j_k}))\leq\int_{[t_1,t_2]}\| V_t\|(\partial_t\phi(.,t))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t).
	\end{equation*}
	Next, for $\ell=0,1,\ldots,j_k2^{p_{j_k}}$ and $t\in[0,j_k]$, we set
	\begin{equation*}
		\tilde\phi_t^{j_k}=\tilde\phi(.,\ell\Delta t_{j_k})\qquad\textup{if}\qquad t\in( (\ell-1)\Delta t_{j_k},\ell\Delta t_{j_k} ].
	\end{equation*}
	Then \cref{in1:9.15} becomes
	\begin{equation*}
		\limsup_k\sum_{\ell=N_1}^{N_2}\Delta t_{j_k}(\delta_\tau (T^{j_k,\ell},\tilde\phi(.\ell\Delta t_{j_k}))(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T^{j_k,\ell}))+\varepsilon_{j_k}^{1/8})\leq\int_{[t_1,t_2]}\limsup_k \delta_\tau (T_t^{j_k},\tilde\phi_t^{j_k})(h_{\tau,\varepsilon_{j_k}}(T_t^{j_k}))\textcolor{black}{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{L}(t).
	\end{equation*}
	Exactly the same argument as on \cite[p.\ 129]{KT17} finishes the proof.
